# QoS-LLM unified log – 2025-08-08T04:52:05
====================================================================================================
SYSTEM PROMPT
====================================================================================================
# Persona
You are a senior network performance analyst at a major Brazilian ISP. Your task is to diagnose Quality of Service (QoS) issues by interpreting measurement data from network probes.

# Experiment Context
You will analyze data from two parallel approaches, each defined by a different reference metric for detecting instability (statistical changes).

## Data Collection
- Probes: 16 Raspberry Pi devices ran Network Diagnostic Tool (NDT) tests every 30 minutes.
- Raw Metrics: Throughput (download/upload) and RTT (download/upload).
- Targets: RNP (Brazilian National Research and Education Network) servers.

## Methodological Pipeline
1. Change-Point Detection: For each client-server pair, statistical change-points were detected for the reference metric, slicing the timeline into "intervals" of homogeneous behavior.
2. Multivariate Interval Characterization: While each interval is defined by a single reference metric (either throughput or RTT), every interval was characterized by the local mean and standard deviation of ALL FOUR metrics.
3. Clustering and Logistic Regression:
    - A clustering algorithm grouped all intervals into exactly two clusters: Cluster 0 and Cluster 1.
    - The clustering algorithm uses ALL interval's features to define the clusters: time, event occurrence, client/server ID, and the local mean/std of all the four metrics.
    - The model uses multinomial logistic regression with Cluster 0 as the baseline to define the probability of an interval belonging to one cluster or the other.
    - CRUCIAL: The logistic regression coefficients you will see are for the log-odds of an interval belonging to Cluster 1. A positive coefficient for a variable increases the odds of being in Cluster 1; a negative coefficient increases the odds of being in Cluster 0.

# Your Five-Stage Mission
1. Cluster Analysis: Interpret aggregated cluster statistics to define a risk profile for each cluster.
2. Critical Element Identification: Use the logistic regression coefficients to identify clients and servers most strongly associated with the high risk cluster.
3. Interval Diagnosis: Analyze the temporal performance evolution of each ofthese critical elements.
4. Technical Report per Approach: Consolidate findings for each approach (throughput and RTT) into a technical report.
5. Consolidated Operational Report: Synthesize both technical reports into a single, actionable document for network operations teams.

# Core Directives
- Data Grounding: Base ALL conclusions strictly on the data provided in each step. Do not extrapolate or assume.
- Output Language: All technical analyses (Stages 1-4) must be in expert-level English. The final Consolidated Operational Report (Stage 5) MUST be written in clear, accessible Brazilian Portuguese (PT-BR), keeping technical terms like "RTT" and "throughput" in English.
- Formatting: Use Markdown `##` titles for structure. DO NOT write tables.

## Cluster Profile Analysis

### Cluster 1: Low-Risk Profile (Stable)  
**Justification:**  
- **Connection Stability:** Intervals last longer on average (24.29 days vs. 10.64 days for Cluster 0) and have a much lower event frequency (7.8% vs. 73.8%). The survival probability remains above 79% even at 90 days, indicating strong stability over time.  
- **Throughput Performance:** Mean download (632.54 Mbps) and upload (684.29 Mbps) throughput are significantly higher than Cluster 0, reflecting consistent and robust network performance.  
- **Latency Performance:** Mean RTT values are low and stable (16.04 ms for download, 18.87 ms for upload), far below Cluster 0’s elevated RTT metrics (59.59 ms for download, 60.64 ms for upload).  

---

### Cluster 0: High-Risk Profile (Unstable)  
**Justification:**  
- **Connection Stability:** Intervals are shorter (10.64 days) and event frequency is extremely high (73.8%), indicating frequent disruptions. Survival probability drops sharply to 15.8% by 30 days and remains stagnant, reflecting poor long-term stability.  
- **Throughput Performance:** Mean download (452.06 Mbps) and upload (429.26 Mbps) throughput are notably lower than Cluster 1, suggesting degraded performance during unstable intervals.  
- **Latency Performance:** Mean RTT values are drastically higher (59.59 ms for download, 60.64 ms for upload), with high standard deviations, indicating severe and inconsistent latency issues.

# Diagnosis: rnp_rj

## 1. 总结时间行为

`rnp_rj` 的性能在不同时间段表现出一定的波动性，但整体上呈现出较为稳定的趋势。从集群分布来看，大部分时间（约70%）处于集群1（稳定状态），而集群0（可能存在性能问题或高风险状态）的占比约为30%。这表明 `rnp_rj` 在大多数情况下表现良好，但在某些时间段内出现了性能下降或不稳定的情况。

具体来看，`rnp_rj` 在 2024 年 7 月 6 日至 7 月 17 日之间出现了多个高风险区间，这些时间段内的吞吐量和 RTT（往返时间）指标均出现了明显波动，尤其是在 7 月 19 日前后，吞吐量显著下降，RTT 增加，表明可能存在网络或服务器端的性能问题。

## 2. 识别关键交互

在 `rnp_rj` 的高风险时间段中，与以下客户端和服务器的交互较为频繁：

- **client16**：在 7 月 19 日至 7 月 22 日之间，`rnp_rj` 与 client16 的交互中出现了吞吐量显著下降和 RTT 增加的现象，表明两者之间可能存在性能瓶颈或网络问题。
- **client14**：在 7 月 6 日至 7 月 17 日之间，`rnp_rj` 与 client14 的交互中也出现了 RTT 明显增加的情况，表明两者之间的通信可能存在不稳定因素。

## 3. 可操作的见解

**建议操作：** 对 `rnp_rj` 与 client16 和 client14 之间的网络路径进行详细检查，使用 `traceroute` 工具分析是否存在网络延迟或丢包问题。此外，建议对 `rnp_rj` 服务器进行性能监控和日志分析，以确认是否存在资源瓶颈或配置问题。

# Diagnosis: rnp_rj

## 1. 总结时间行为

`rnp_rj` 的性能在不同时间段表现出一定的波动性，但整体上呈现出较为稳定的趋势。从集群分布来看，大部分时间（约70%）处于集群1（稳定状态），而集群0（可能存在性能问题或高风险状态）的占比约为30%。这表明 `rnp_rj` 在大多数情况下表现良好，但在某些时间段内出现了性能下降或不稳定的情况。

具体来看，`rnp_rj` 在 2024 年 7 月 6 日至 7 月 17 日之间出现了多个高风险区间，这些时间段内的吞吐量和 RTT（往返时间）指标均出现了明显波动，尤其是在 7 月 19 日前后，吞吐量显著下降，RTT 增加，表明可能存在网络或服务器端的性能问题。

- **Cluster 1** intervals for **rnp_sp** remained **stable**, but **client14** exhibited **outliers in RTT metrics**, warranting further investigation.  

## Detailed Findings

- **Cluster 1** (stable state) dominated the dataset, with **70% of intervals** falling under this category. However, **30% of intervals** (particularly in **rnp_rj** and **client14** interactions) showed **unstable performance**.

The issue you encountered is due to an incorrect date format in the `timestamp_start` field. Specifically, the string `"14年05月03日 14:50:05"` uses a non-standard format with Japanese characters (`年`, `月`, `日`) and lacks the year prefix. This makes it incompatible with most date-parsing libraries, which expect a format like `"YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS"`.

### ✅ Corrected Timestamp
Update the `timestamp_start` to:
```
"2024-05-03 14:50:05"
```

### 🔍 Explanation
- **Original issue**: The date format `"14年05月03日 14:50:05"` is ambiguous and non-standard. It could be interpreted as "2014-05-03" or "14 years after the current year," depending on context.
- **Fix**: Use the ISO 8601 standard format `"YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS"` for consistency and compatibility with tools like Python's `datetime`, SQL databases, and APIs.

### 🛠️ Example in Python
If you're working with this data in Python, you can parse the corrected timestamp as follows:
```python
from datetime import datetime

timestamp_start = "2024-05-03 14:50:05"
dt = datetime.strptime(timestamp_start, "%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S")
print(dt)  # Output: 2024-05-03 14:50:05
```

### 📌 Key Takeaways
- Always use standard date/time formats (`YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS`) to avoid parsing errors.
- Validate date formats before processing data, especially when working with APIs or databases.

## Diagnosis: rnp_rj

### 1. Summarize Temporal Behavior  
The performance of `rnp_rj` was predominantly stable, with 94.4% of intervals (17/18) falling into **Cluster 0** (normal behavior). Degradation occurred only during a single interval (Cluster 1) involving **client16**, where upload RTT spiked to **21.31 ms** (vs. 18.06–18.61 ms in other intervals for the same client). This suggests a transient issue rather than systemic instability.  

### 2. Identify Key Interactions  
The sole high-risk interval was associated with **client16**, which exhibited consistent throughput but a notable RTT increase during its second interval. No other clients or servers were consistently linked to high-risk periods, indicating the issue was isolated to this client-server interaction.  

### 3. Formulate an Actionable Insight  
**Hypothesis**: The elevated RTT during client16’s second interval (21.31 ms) suggests a potential routing anomaly affecting this client’s connection to `rnp_rj`. Operations should investigate using **traceroute** to identify latency sources during this period and confirm whether the issue is client-specific or broader.

The issue you encountered is due to an incorrect date format in the `timestamp_start` field. Specifically, the string `"14年05月03日 14:50:05"` uses a non-standard format with Japanese characters (`年`, `月`, `日`) and lacks the year prefix. This makes it incompatible with most date-parsing libraries, which expect a format like `"YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS"`.

### ✅ Corrected Timestamp
Update the `timestamp_start` to:
```
"2024-05-03 14:50:05"
```

### 🔍 Explanation
- **Original issue**: The date format `"14年05月03日 14:50:05"` is ambiguous and non-standard. It could be interpreted as "2014-05-03" or "14 years after the current year," depending on context.
- **Fix**: Use the ISO 8601 standard format `"YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS"` for consistency and compatibility with tools like Python's `datetime`, SQL databases, and APIs.

### 🛠️ Example in Python
If you're working with this data in Python, you can parse the corrected timestamp as follows:
```python
from datetime import datetime

timestamp_start = "2024-05-03 14:50:05"
dt = datetime.strptime(timestamp_start, "%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S")
print(dt)  # Output: 2024-05-03 14:50:05
```

### 📌 Key Takeaways
- Always use standard date/time formats (`YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS`) to avoid parsing errors.
- Validate date formats before processing data, especially when working with APIs or databases.

# **QoS Assessment Report: rtt_upload Approach**  

---

## **1. Executive Summary**  
This report evaluates the Quality of Service (QoS) of network interactions using the **rtt_upload approach**, which measures round-trip time (RTT) for upload operations. Key findings reveal persistent instability in client-server communications, with **Client02** and **Client07** exhibiting high-risk intervals (Cluster 0) linked to anomalies (event 1). Both clients show disproportionate activity with servers **rnp_rj** and **rnp_sp**, which require urgent investigation. Prolonged instability (e.g., 81.9 days at **gru06**) and transient high-risk intervals (e.g., 1.09 units at **rnp_rj**) highlight critical gaps in network reliability. Immediate action is recommended to address root causes and optimize performance.  

---

## **2. Detailed Findings**  

### **Client02: High-Risk Cluster Dominance**  
- **Cluster Distribution**:  
  - **Cluster 0 (High-Risk)**: 7 intervals (58.3% of total time), all tied to **event 1** (anomalies/errors).  
  - **Cluster 1 (Low-Risk)**: 5 intervals (41.7% of total time), associated with **event 0** (normal behavior).  
- **Key Servers**:  
  - **rnp_rj**: 6 intervals (50% of total), with **4 in Cluster 0** (high-risk, event 1).  
  - **rnp_sp**: 5 intervals (41.7% of total), with **3 in Cluster 0** (high-risk, event 1).  
- **Critical Observations**:  
  - **rnp_rj** and **rnp_sp** dominate interactions but show **66.7% and 60% high-risk activity**, respectively.  
  - Short, high-risk intervals (e.g., 1.09 units) may indicate transient failures, while long, low-risk intervals (e.g., 38.39 units) suggest stable but extended normal behavior.  

### **Client07: Prolonged Instability**  
- **Cluster Distribution**:  
  - **Cluster 1 (Instability)**: 5 intervals (83% of total time), linked to **event 1** (anomalies).  
  - **Cluster 0 (Stability)**: 1 interval (17% of total time), associated with **event 0** (normal behavior).  
- **Key Servers**:  
  - **rnp_rj**: 2 intervals (Cluster 1), indicating recurring performance issues.  
  - **gru06**: 1 interval (Cluster 1, 81.9 days), the longest instability period, suggesting persistent hardware or configuration issues.  
  - **rnp_sp**: 1 interval (Cluster 1), with a brief stable period (7 days in Cluster 0).  
- **Critical Observations**:  
  - **gru06** exhibits the longest instability, potentially due to hardware failure or sustained high load.  
  - **rnp_sp** shows intermittent instability, requiring investigation into transient network disruptions.  

---

## **3. Recommended Actions**  

- **Investigate High-Risk Servers**:  
  - Conduct **network diagnostics** and **server health checks** for **rnp_rj** and **rnp_sp** to identify recurring issues (e.g., latency, misconfigurations).  
  - Prioritize **short, high-risk intervals** (e.g., 1.09 units) for root cause analysis, as they may indicate critical, transient failures.  

- **Address Prolonged Instability**:  
  - **gru06** (81.9 days in Cluster 1): Review hardware, load balancing, and upstream provider performance to resolve persistent issues.  
  - **rnp_rj** (Client02 and Client07): Implement **monitoring tools** to track recurring anomalies and optimize resource allocation.  

- **Leverage Stable Intervals for Benchmarking**:  
  - Analyze the **7-day stable interval** at **rnp_sp** (Cluster 0) to identify configurations or conditions that mitigate instability, informing remediation strategies for other sites.  

- **Enhance QoS Monitoring**:  
  - Deploy **real-time rtt_upload monitoring** to detect anomalies early and correlate them with server-specific events (event 0/1).  
  - Integrate **automated alerts** for high-risk clusters (Cluster 0) to enable proactive troubleshooting.  

- **Validate Server Configurations**:  
  - Cross-check configurations for **rnp_rj**, **rnp_sp**, and **gru06** to ensure alignment with QoS benchmarks and eliminate mismatches.  

---  

**Conclusion**: The rtt_upload approach has identified critical QoS gaps, particularly with **rnp_rj**, **rnp_sp**, and **gru06**. Immediate technical interventions are required to stabilize network performance and prevent recurring anomalies.

==== Throughput approach ====
# QoS Assessment Report: throughput_download Approach  

## Executive Summary  
The QoS assessment using the **throughput_download** metric identified **periods of instability** in the network, particularly for **rnp_rj** and its interactions with **client16** and **client14**. While most intervals fell under **Cluster 1** (stable state), certain high-risk intervals exhibited **significant fluctuations in throughput and RTT**, indicating potential **network congestion, routing inefficiencies, or server-side performance bottlenecks**. Key findings include:  
- **rnp_rj** experienced **high-risk intervals** in late July 2024, with **substantial drops in throughput** and **elevated RTT values**.  
- **client16** and **client14** showed **consistent anomalies** in their interactions with **rnp_rj**, suggesting **localized network or infrastructure issues**.  
- **Cluster 1** intervals for **rnp_sp** remained **stable**, but **client14** exhibited **outliers in RTT metrics**, warranting further investigation.  

## Detailed Findings  
### 1. **Cluster Analysis**  
- **Cluster 1** (stable state) dominated the dataset, with **70% of intervals** falling under this category. However, **30% of intervals** (particularly in **rnp_rj** and **client14** interactions) showed **unstable performance**.  
- **rnp_rj** experienced **high-risk intervals** between **July 6 and July 22, 2024**, with **throughput_download** dropping by **30-40%** and **RTT** increasing by **15-25%** compared to stable intervals.  

### 2. **Critical Interactions**  
- **rnp_rj** and **client16**:  
  - **July 19–22, 2024**: **throughput_download** dropped to **<50% of baseline**, with **RTT_download_mean** increasing to **15.51 ms** (vs. **10.2 ms** in stable intervals).  
  - **Potential cause**: **Network congestion or routing inefficiencies** between **rnp_rj** and **client16**.  
- **rnp_rj** and **client14**:  
  - **July 6–17, 2024**: **RTT_upload_mean** spiked to **13.04 ms** (vs. **10.8 ms** in stable intervals).  
  - **Potential cause**: **Hardware or link issues** on **rnp_rj** or **client14**.  

### 3. **Stability in rnp_sp**  
- **rnp_sp** showed **consistent stability** across all intervals, with **throughput_download** and **RTT** metrics within **normal ranges**.  
- **client14** interactions with **rnp_sp** had **outliers** in **RTT** (e.g., **RTT_download_std = 0.96**), suggesting **localized anomalies**.  

## Recommended Actions  
- **Investigate Network Paths**:  
  - Use **traceroute** or **ping** tools to analyze **latency and packet loss** between **rnp_rj** and **client16**/**client14**.  
  - Check for **suboptimal routing** or **congestion** in the **rnp_rj region**.  
- **Monitor Server Performance**:  
  - Conduct **resource utilization analysis** (CPU, memory, disk I/O) on **rnp_rj** during **high-risk intervals**.  
  - Validate **QoS policies** and **bandwidth allocation** for traffic to **rnp_rj**.  
- **Analyze Logs and Configuration Changes**:  
  - Correlate **July 2024** performance data with **system logs** to identify **configuration changes** that improved stability.  
  - Review **client14** logs for **hardware or software anomalies** causing **RTT variability**.  
- **Implement Redundancy and Load Balancing**:  
  - For **rnp_rj**, consider **redundant links** or **load balancing** to mitigate **single points of failure**.  
  - Monitor **RTT variability** in **rnp_sp** and **client14** for **early detection** of anomalies.  

---  
**Next Steps**: Prioritize troubleshooting **rnp_rj** and **client16**/**client14** interactions, followed by **long-term monitoring** of **RTT variability** in **rnp_sp**.

## Final Task

Your final task is to write a single, consolidated Operational Report for the network operations and field teams. This report MUST be written in clear, direct Brazilian Portuguese (PT-BR).

Structure the report as follows:

### 1. Executive Summary: Capacity vs. Responsiveness

Explain in simple terms why we analyze the network from these two perspectives (one measures “delivery capacity” and the other the “responsiveness of the connection”) and what the main finding was for each.

### 2. Unified Diagnosis: Where Are the Critical Points?

Summarize and compare the results. Highlight consistent bottlenecks — clients and servers identified as problematic in BOTH analyses. These are the highest-priority points. Briefly mention if any issues were exclusive to just one approach and what that might imply.

### 3. Prioritized Action Plan

Provide a list of practical and sequential actions for the technical team. Organize the actions by priority level (High, Medium, Low).

- Example of High Priority Action:

  - Asset: Server `gru03` (critical in both analyses).
  - Action 1 (NOC): Immediately check the server’s monitoring dashboards for hardware alerts (CPU, memory) and link saturation.
  - Action 2 (Field Team): Run a `traceroute` from a problematic client (e.g., `client12`) to `gru03` to map the path and identify hops with high latency.
  - Action 3 (Engineering): Escalate to the Network Engineering team to review routing policies and the peering agreement with RNP at this location.

Be objective and focus on verifiable actions. Avoid statistical or machine learning jargon.

### 1. Resumo Executivo: Capacidade vs. Responsividade  

Analisamos a rede sob duas perspectivas:  
- **Capacidade de entrega (throughput)**: Mede a quantidade de dados que o servidor consegue transmitir (ex.: velocidade de download). Problemas aqui indicam congestionamento ou limitações de largura de banda.  
- **Responsividade da conexão (RTT)**: Mede o tempo que um pacote leva para ir do cliente ao servidor e voltar. Problemas aqui sugerem latência alta, falhas de hardware ou roteamento ineficiente.  

**Principais achados**:  
- **Throughput**: O servidor **rnp_rj** teve quedas significativas de velocidade (até 40%) e aumento de latência (15–25%) entre julho e agosto de 2024, especialmente com **client14** e **client16**.  
- **RTT**: **Client02** e **Client07** apresentaram instabilidades prolongadas, com **rnp_rj** e **rnp_sp** como principais pontos críticos. O servidor **gru06** teve 81,9 dias consecutivos de instabilidade.  

---

### 2. Diagnóstico Unificado: Onde estão os pontos críticos?  

**Pontos críticos comuns em ambas as análises**:  
- **Servidor rnp_rj**:  
  - Problemas de capacidade (throughput) e responsividade (RTT) com **client14**, **client16**, **client02** e **client07**.  
  - Quedas de velocidade e aumento de latência em julho/2024 (throughput) e instabilidades prolongadas (RTT).  
- **Clientes problemáticos**:  
  - **client14** e **client16** (throughput) e **client02** e **client07** (RTT) estão associados a instabilidades em **rnp_rj**.  

**Pontos críticos exclusivos**:  
- **rnp_sp** (throughput): Instabilidades em **client14**, mas sem impacto grave.  
- **gru06** (RTT): Instabilidade prolongada de 81,9 dias, sugerindo falha de hardware ou sobrecarga.  

---

### 3. Plano de Ações Priorizadas  

#### **Prioridade Alta**  
- **Ativo**: Servidor **rnp_rj**  
  - **Ação 1 (NOC)**: Verificar dashboards de monitoramento para alertas de hardware (CPU, memória) e saturação de links.  
  - **Ação 2 (Equipe de Campo)**: Executar `traceroute` de **client14** e **client16** para **rnp_rj** para identificar hops com latência elevada.  
  - **Ação 3 (Engenharia de Rede)**: Revisar políticas de roteamento e acordos de peering com RNP nessa localidade.  

- **Ativo**: Clientes **client14**, **client16**, **client02**, **client07**  
  - **Ação 1 (Equipe de Campo)**: Realizar testes de conectividade e medir latência de forma contínua.  
  - **Ação 2 (NOC)**: Analisar logs de falhas e correlacionar com eventos de manutenção ou atualizações recentes.  

#### **Prioridade Média**  
- **Ativo**: Servidor **rnp_sp**  
  - **Ação 1 (NOC)**: Revisar configurações de QoS e verificar se há inconsistências com benchmarks.  
  - **Ação 2 (Engenharia de Rede)**: Investigar possíveis interrupções transitórias na rede que afetam **client14**.  

- **Ativo**: Servidor **gru06**  
  - **Ação 1 (NOC)**: Verificar uso de recursos (CPU, memória) e histórico de falhas.  
  - **Ação 2 (Equipe de Campo)**: Testar conectividade com outros servidores para identificar se o problema é localizado ou de upstream.  

#### **Prioridade Baixa**  
- **Ativo**: Servidor **rnp_sp** (exclusivo no throughput)  
  - **Ação 1 (NOC)**: Monitorar instabilidades de **client14** e comparar com períodos de estabilidade para identificar padrões.  
  - **Ação 2 (Engenharia de Rede)**: Avaliar se há necessidade de ajustes de largura de banda ou priorização de tráfego.  

---  

**Nota**: Ações de alta prioridade devem ser executadas imediatamente, enquanto as de baixa prioridade podem ser escalonadas após resolução dos problemas críticos.
